# Decision log (2026-08-19 → 2026-09-13)

> Archived verbatim from PROGRESS.md on 2026-09-14 (development-system v2 restructure). Append-only history; grep it, never read it in full.


- 2026-09-13 -- OWNER DECISION, UI step 1 wrap-up: qa gate for the app shell was STOPPED mid-run by owner instruction (time/effort), before it wrote a verdict -- recorded here so the ledger doesn't imply a qa PASS. Shell kept rather than reverted: it passed tester and security-reviewer, no failure is attributed to it (the tester's E2E failures were root-caused to the sign-in rate limit), and reverting would cost more effort for no gain; it is explicitly interim and will be replaced by the design-system work. Sequencing changed from the earlier plan: next is the owner-directed design-system build (owner supplies the process), then the remaining Phase 2-4 features, then applying the design system to screens. Cleanup: 109 throwaway `@example.com` test users created by this session's tester/qa runs deleted from the shared dev project (profiles under erasure_in_progress, then GoTrue admin delete). Push access: the GitHub account first signed in on this Mac ("retrospeq") got 403 on lucedge/Retrospeq_v1; owner signed in as `lucedge` via `gh auth login` + `gh auth setup-git` the same day and the push succeeded (c389cf6..df32bff) -- resolved.
- 2026-09-13 -- SECURITY REVIEW (retrospeq-security-reviewer), GATE
  (blocking authority) on "UI phase step 1 -- app shell" (commits
  `622a56f` + `d3dc898`, on top of TESTER's PASS above). **Verdict:
  PASS.** Ran independently on macOS/Node 24 against the already-running
  dev server; ignored the ledger's Windows/C:-drive workarounds per this
  dispatch's own environment note.

  1. **Auth/aal2 gate unchanged and inherited by both new routes,
  proven not assumed.** `app/(app)/layout.tsx` lines 39-61
  (`getUser`, `getAuthenticatorAssuranceLevel`, the
  `aal.nextLevel === 'aal2' && aal.currentLevel !== 'aal2'` redirect,
  fail-toward-`/mfa-challenge` on AAL-check error) is unchanged from the
  pre-`622a56f` version -- only the JSX below the guard changed. `/settings`
  and `/performance` are new `page.tsx` files under `app/(app)/`, so they
  render as `{children}` inside this same layout with no route-local
  override; confirmed live with unauthenticated curl against the running
  dev server: `curl -i http://localhost:3000/settings` and `.../performance`
  both returned `307` to `location: /login`, identical to the
  already-reviewed `/dashboard` baseline (`curl -i .../dashboard` same
  result). No bypass exists.

  2. **`AppShellNav.tsx` -- no data leak, no server-only import in the
  client bundle.** `'use client'` component; its only imports are
  `next/link` and `next/navigation` (`usePathname`). It receives no
  props and reads nothing but `window`-derived pathname -- no user data,
  no Supabase client, no server module. `sectionFor`/`TABS`/
  `RULEBOOK_LINKS` are static route tables, not data fetched from
  anywhere. `SettingsLink`/`TabBar`/`RulebookSubnav` render only static
  labels/icons/hrefs. No new client-reachable data path introduced.

  3. **Sign-out unchanged.** `app/(app)/settings/page.tsx` imports the
  same `signOut` from `app/(auth)/actions.ts` the old header used
  (`export async function signOut(): Promise<void> { ... await
  supabase.auth.signOut(); redirect('/login'); }`, byte-identical to
  pre-`622a56f`), invoked via `<form action={signOut}>` -- a React
  Server Action form submission (POST, not a GET link), same mechanism
  as before, just relocated from the header to `/settings`, which itself
  sits behind the same auth+aal2 guard verified in (1). No new open-
  redirect surface (redirect target is the hardcoded `/login`, not
  derived from any request input) and no CSRF regression vs. the prior
  form.

  4. **`devIndicators: false` is not security-relevant.** Checked
  against this repo's actual vendored Next.js docs (per AGENTS.md's "not
  the Next.js you know" instruction), not assumption:
  `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/
  01-next-config-js/devIndicators.md` -- this option only hides the
  dev-mode on-screen route-info badge; the doc states explicitly "Next.js
  will still surface any compile or runtime errors that were
  encountered." It is a `next dev`-only visual affordance with no
  production behaviour and no effect on error overlays/traces.

  5. **Service-role allowlist unaffected.** `grep -rn
  "withServiceRoleConnection\|createServiceRoleClient"` across all four
  changed/new files (`app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `app/(app)/AppShellNav.tsx`,
  `app/(app)/settings/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/performance/page.tsx`):
  zero hits -- only `createClient` (the ordinary user-scoped Supabase
  client) appears, in `layout.tsx`'s existing auth guard. No new entry
  needed in `lib/supabase/__tests__/service-role-inventory.test.ts`'s
  allowlist. Ran the test itself to confirm the existing allowlist still
  holds: `npx vitest run lib/supabase/__tests__/service-role-inventory.test.ts`
  -> 3/3 passed.

  6. **No new env reads, no new request-body/API-route surface, no Zod
  boundary needed.** This slice adds no Server Action, no API route, and
  no form beyond the pre-existing `signOut` (which takes no input). The
  CSS diff (`public/brand/css/components.css` / vendored
  `retrospeq-design-system/brand/css/components.css`, tab-bar sizing +
  `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`) is presentation-only, no security
  surface.

  No blocking findings. Module's security bar for this slice: clear.

  **1. Auth/aal2 gate untouched, confirmed two ways.** Read the actual
  diff: `AppLayout`'s body (lines 39-61 -- `getUser`, `getAuthenticator
  AssuranceLevel`, the `aal.nextLevel === 'aal2' && aal.currentLevel !==
  'aal2'` redirect, the fail-toward-`/mfa-challenge` posture on AAL-check

  **2. `sectionFor` route coverage -- no gap found.** Cross-checked

  (`find "app/(app)" -name page.tsx`): every one
  of the 22 real `page.tsx` routes (`/accounts`, `/accounts/[id]/

  **3. Full E2E suite -- root-caused a mass failure as environmental, not
  shell-caused, with direct reproduction, not a guess.** First full run
  (`npx playwright test`, 5 workers): **17 passed / 61 failed** (78
  total). 3 of the 61 are the already-known, already-documented
  Supabase-mailer gap (`NEEDS_YOUR_INPUT.md` -- `auth.spec.ts` signup/
  reset-request). The other 58 were NOT obviously pre-existing, so I
  built a standalone diagnostic (`Client`+`chromium`, real admin-API
  user, real DB seed, screenshot) reproducing one of them
  (`rules-adherence.spec.ts`'s `.adherence` "element(s) not found") in
  isolation -- **the actual rendered page showed "Too many attempts.
  Please wait a few minutes and try again."** in place of the adherence
  section (screenshot: was at scratch path, not retained -- the finding
  is the reproduced text, not the file). That string traces to
  `lib/rate-limit/limiter.ts`/`lib/rate-limit/config.ts`'s real,
  pre-existing, Module 01 §7.2-mandated IP-based rate limits (`signin:
  {ip: {limit: 20, windowSeconds: 900}}`, plus per-action limits like
  `RULE_RATE_LIMITED`) -- NOT anything shell/markup-related. 54 of the 61
  failures were `waitForURL` timeouts waiting for navigation away from
  `/login` (sign-in never completed = rate-limited before the click even
  mattered); the remaining ~7 (including the one directly reproduced)
  are downstream "expected content missing, got the rate-limit fallback
  message" failures. **Zero of the 61 failures showed the specific
  regression signature this dispatch was asked to hunt for** -- grepped
  the full failure log for `strict mode`/`resolved to [2-9] element`/
  multi-match `Sign out` collisions: none found.

  Given this, I waited out the 15-minute IP-wide signin window
  (confirmed via the limiter's own `windowSeconds`/epoch-bucket math)
  and re-ran a sample of the originally-failing tests, serially, with
  ZERO code changes, specifically prioritising shell-relevant/highest-
  risk ones: `dashboard.spec.ts` (4/4, including the shell-added "Home
  tab" test AND the `.adherence`-bearing "Clear" test that had failed in
  the mass run), `onboarding.spec.ts` (3/3), `strategy-detail.
  independent-verify.spec.ts` (4/4 -- including the one test this commit
  itself edited, sign-out now via `/settings`: confirmed working
  end-to-end through the real UI, not just that the file compiles),
  `rules-list.spec.ts` (6/6). **17/17 flipped from FAIL to PASS with no
  code changes** -- decisive evidence the cascade is the rate limiter,
  not this commit.

  **Flagged gap, reported honestly, not papered over:** the remaining
  ~44 of the 61 originally-failed tests were NOT individually re-run in
  isolation this session -- getting the above 17 re-verified plus the
  diagnostic already consumed two full 15-minute IP-wide rate-limit
  windows, and re-verifying the rest one spec-file-batch per window
  would have taken several more. Every one of those ~44 failure messages
  matches one of the two confirmed root-cause signatures (login timeout
  or rate-limit-fallback content) and none show the shell-regression
  signature searched for above, so I have high confidence they are the
  same root cause -- but "high confidence by pattern-match" is not the
  same as "independently re-run and confirmed," and I am not marking
  them PASS. **New standing finding for whoever owns E2E infra next:**
  this suite (78 tests, most doing a real `signInWithPassword`) is now
  large enough that running it in one parallel (or even fully serial)
  shot structurally exceeds Module 01 §7.2's own 20-signins/900s
  per-IP limit regardless of code correctness -- this is not new to this
  commit, but this is the first time it's been directly diagnosed and
  written down. Recommend either a test-environment-only rate-limit
  carve-out (env-gated, never touching the production limit) or running
  the suite in rate-limit-respecting batches going forward; did not
  implement either myself since both are a product/infra decision, not
  this gate's call.

- 2026-09-13 -- OWNER DECISION, visual design authority + UI plan. `retrospeq-design-system/brand/` (amber Instrument system, `brand/docs/instrument.html` mockup) is authoritative for all visual decisions; `modules/09-design-system.md`'s indigo accent / IBM Plex / shadcn/ui / Phosphor / dark-primary choices are superseded (00-foundation §12 reconciliation: the two specs disagreed, nothing had recorded which wins; agents had de facto followed `brand/`). Root cause of the "ugly UI" finding logged for the record: no phase ever scheduled a visual build, every slice shipped just-enough UI for its feature, and qa's checklist verified rules (no red/green, one `.rq-btn`) but never mockup fidelity -- zero of the design system's marks are used anywhere in `app/`. Fix: sequencing (shell now, features, then a dedicated UI phase -- Phase table "UI" row) plus a standing mockup-fidelity line in the coder and qa agent definitions so new screens stop adding to the rework. The four-tab structure itself is Module 08 §7.5, not new product scope; `/settings` is a new route grouping existing screens, `/performance` is an honest not-built state.
Format: `YYYY-MM-DD — decision — why — spec/section it reconciles`

- 2026-09-13 -- ORCHESTRATOR, process change: commit per-gate-pass, not
  only once at the end of a slice's full coder -> tester -> security-
  reviewer -> qa chain. Owner-requested directly (this machine's VS
  Code/session has crashed and restarted mid-chain repeatedly this
  build -- see [[host-memory-pressure-vscode-crashes]] memory entry --
  and hours of multi-gate work sitting uncommitted is real risk even
  though the underlying files themselves survive a session crash,
  since the owner's own stated preference is fewer, smaller windows of
  uncommitted work, not just "the files are technically safe"). From
  now on: after tester PASS, commit; after security-reviewer PASS (or
  a security-reviewer-required fix cycle's own final re-verification
  PASS), commit again; after qa PASS, final commit. This does NOT lower
  this repo's own "not marked done until qa passes" bar -- PROGRESS.md's
  AT A GLANCE/Phase-status framing still only calls a slice DONE after
  qa, and an interim commit's own message will say so plainly (e.g.
  "not yet qa-reviewed") -- it only means the same total set of commits
  a slice always produced arrives spread across the chain instead of
  squashed into one commit at the very end. Every commit still only
  ever contains code that has passed at least the coder's own self-
  check and (from the tester-pass commit onward) real independent
  verification -- this is strictly more frequent checkpointing of the
  exact same quality bar, not a relaxation of it. Applied starting with
  Module 06 Slice 6 (coder -> tester -> two real fix cycles ->
  security-reviewer PASS already complete at the time of this note;
  committing now rather than waiting on the qa gate already in flight).

- 2026-09-13 -- CODER (retrospeq-coder), FIX for the one real gap the
  same-day `retrospeq-tester` gate found in Module 06 Slice 6
  (`## 2026-09-13 -- Module 06 (Review & Graduation) Slice 6 -- TESTER
  GATE` entry above/below in this same log): `lib/review/decisions/
  graduation-operand-map.ts`'s `resolveOperandForField` treated
  `drv.order_type` as a fifth working field alongside `risk_pct`/
  `hold_seconds`/`day_of_week`/`instrument`, but `order_type`'s own
  `lib/rules/operand-catalogue.ts` entry is `computableToday: false` (no
  `order_type` column exists anywhere in Module 02's schema). Before this
  fix, accepting a graduation prompt on `drv.order_type` would have
  created a real `rules` row that could never actually evaluate --
  strictly worse than this same file's own honest "can't become a rule
  yet" rejection, since it looks like success.

  **Fix, and why this shape over a one-line exclusion**: rather than a
  one-off `if (fieldId === 'drv.order_type') return null` special case,
  `resolveOperandForField` now checks `operand.computableToday` generically
  for ALL five mapped fields after resolving via
  `DERIVED_FIELD_TO_OPERAND_ID` -- one `if (!operand.computableToday)
  return null` after the existing lookup, no new control flow, no new
  parameter. Chosen over the narrower exclusion because the failure mode
  this bug represents ("a mapped field's catalogue entry silently stops
  being evaluable") is not specific to `order_type` -- nothing prevents a
  future, honest downgrade of `risk_pct`/`hold_seconds`/`day_of_week`/
  `instrument`'s own `computableToday` (e.g. if a fact-assembly
  regression were ever found and the entry corrected), and a one-off
  exclusion would not catch that; the generic check does, automatically,
  with no second bug report required. `drv.order_type` now falls through
  to the IDENTICAL rejection path (`GRADUATION_FIELD_UNSUPPORTED`, "This
  kind of finding can't become a rule yet.") already used for every
  out-of-scope custom field (e.g. "conviction") -- confirmed by reading
  `app/(app)/review/decisions/actions.ts`'s existing `if (!operand)`
  branch, which needed no changes at all since it already treats any
  `null` from `resolveOperandForField` as this one honest outcome.

  **Verification**: `lib/review/decisions/__tests__/graduation-operand-
  map.test.ts` updated -- the old "GENUINE GAP, FLAGGED NOT FIXED" test
  (which asserted `drv.order_type` DID resolve, documenting the bug) is
  replaced with a test asserting it now resolves to `null`, plus a new
  test explicitly asserting all four remaining fields still resolve with
  `computableToday: true` (the "doesn't break the other 4" check this fix
  was explicitly asked to prove). Full pass, real runs, not assumed:
  - `graduation-operand-map.test.ts` + `app/(app)/review/decisions/
    __tests__/actions.test.ts`: 44/44 pass.
  - `lib/review/decisions/__tests__/decisions-read-path.live.test.ts` +
    `app/(app)/review/decisions/__tests__/decisions-integration.live
    .test.ts` (real writes/reads against the shared dev Supabase project,
    ADR 0002) rerun in full: 12/12 pass, including the real `drv.risk_pct`
    accept-with-real-writes scenario and the custom-field/defer/cross-
    user-isolation scenarios -- none of these exercised `order_type`
    before or after, and all still pass unchanged.
  - `tsc --noEmit`: clean. `eslint` (scoped to the two touched files):
    clean. `npm run check:import-boundaries`: clean (104 modules, 266
    dependencies, no violations).
  - A full combined `lib/review lib/rules app/(app)/review app/(app)/rules`
    run was ALSO attempted (both default multi-worker and
    `--pool=forks --poolOptions.forks.singleFork=true`) as a broader
    regression sweep, per this fix's own instruction to check "everything
    else touching this file." The default-pool attempt hit this machine's
    known standing memory-pressure infra gap (`Fatal process out of
    memory: Zone`, consistent with prior sessions' documented ~5-6GB
    free-RAM ceiling under multi-worker load). Two single-fork retries
    were run instead -- one scoped to `lib/rules` only, one the full
    combined set -- and BOTH eventually completed as background tasks
    (941s and 1659s respectively; they were not intentionally run
    concurrently, but their runtimes did overlap for part of that window,
    an avoidable overlap noted for future sessions rather than repeated):
    - `lib/rules`-only: 44/47 files, 580/587 tests passed.
    - Full combined set: **73/76 files, 923/927 tests passed.**
    Both runs' only failures are the SAME three files every time --
    `adherence-repository.live.test.ts`, `severity-lifecycle.live.test.ts`,
    `severity-lifecycle.independent-verification.live.test.ts` (Module 04
    Slices 6/7) -- which have ZERO import relationship to `graduation-
    operand-map.ts`/`resolveOperandForField` (confirmed by the same
    import grep used throughout this fix). The failure signature differs
    slightly between the two runs (plain 30s/150s test timeouts in the
    combined run; timeouts PLUS a Postgres "deadlock detected" and a
    cascade of "current transaction is aborted" in the run that
    genuinely overlapped with the combined run's tail end) but both
    point the same direction: these three files' own live-DB
    concurrency/promotion tests are slow and/or contend for locks on
    shared tables (`rules`/`rule_evaluations`/`trades`/
    `adherence_weekly`) against this shared dev Supabase project (ADR
    0002) under current conditions, independent of whether another
    vitest process happens to be running at the same time -- not
    something this fix's own change (a pure read of `computableToday`
    inside `resolveOperandForField`) could plausibly cause or worsen.
    Every file this fix actually relates to within `lib/rules` --
    `operand-catalogue.test.ts` (15/15), `editable-operands.test.ts`
    (8/8), `validate-operand-op-value.test.ts` (33/33), `cross-trade-
    operand-values.test.ts` (57/57) -- and every file under `lib/review`/
    `app/(app)/rules` (including `rules/actions.test.ts`, 102/102, which
    covers `createRule`'s `origin` handling this fix's caller depends on)
    passed cleanly in both runs. Flagged here per this repo's own "flag
    it, don't paper over it" convention rather than silently omitted, but
    NOT logged as a new regression introduced by this fix -- if these
    three files' own flakiness is worth investigating on its own, that is
    a separate, pre-existing Module 04 Slice 6/7 concern for whichever
    session picks it up next, not part of this fix's scope or blocking
    its handoff.

  **Does this close the tester's finding?** Yes -- the one real, tester-
  found defect (a rule that would be created but never evaluate) no
  longer occurs; `drv.order_type` now produces the identical honest
  outcome as any other unsupported field, proven by a direct test
  assertion, not just code inspection.

  **Status for security-reviewer**: this fix is ready to be picked up
  alongside the rest of Slice 6. It does NOT touch, and does not resolve,
  ADR 0040 decision 7's separately-flagged `origin` bypass gap
  (`createRule` accepting a caller-supplied `origin: 'graduated'` with no
  gate on which caller may use it) -- that remains exactly as flagged,

  constant in `promotion.ts`, id-keyed and ORed into
  `evaluateShadowToBetaPromotion` so a future call site that omits the
  `{ permanentlyShadow: true }` option entirely still cannot promote this

  the section 8 render-rate metric (`< 5% of users`) has real data to

  **A real, load-bearing regression found and fixed before handoff, not
  left for the tester to discover**: the new sync.ts wiring is one more
  sequential, awaited DB round trip in a post-sync hook chain that
  `lib/ingestion/__tests__/sync.live.test.ts` already ran close to its
  own per-test timeout ceiling on this machine (several pre-existing
  tests were already clocking 18-21s with NO explicit timeout override in
  that file at all). Diagnosed with a real A/B comparison, not a guess:
  reverted just `sync.ts` via `git stash` and re-ran the full live-DB
  suite both ways -- two of the heaviest tests (`cross-account isolation`,
  `dedup is per-fill, not per-batch` -- the latter runs THREE full
  sequential `runSync` calls in one test) timed out with the wiring
  restored and were markedly tighter even reverted, confirming both a
  real added cost from this change AND a genuinely pre-existing, unrelated
  margin problem in the same file (the "dedup" test's own multi-sync
  shape timed out even fully reverted, in one full-file run). First tried
  a file-level `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 30_000 })`, this repo's own
  already-established convention for exactly this situation (used
  identically in a dozen other live-DB test files) -- verified via a
  standalone throwaway diagnostic test that the config mechanism itself
  genuinely was taking effect (it was), yet the two real failing tests
  kept citing "20000ms" verbatim regardless of the file-level value, even
  at 30s then 60s. **Actual root cause, found by direct investigation
  rather than a bigger guessed number**: EVERY test in this file already
  carried its OWN explicit per-test timeout (`it(name, fn, 20_000)`, the
  literal third argument, present on all 8 heavier tests before this
  slice) -- Vitest/Jest's own documented precedence is that an explicit
  per-test timeout WINS over a file-level `vi.setConfig`, so the
  file-level bump was doing nothing for the two affected tests. Fixed at
  the actual call sites: the two affected tests' own explicit third
  argument raised from `20_000` to `60_000` (matching
  `trigger-conditions-repository.live.test.ts`'s own existing 60s
  precedent for the identical "several sequential live-DB operations in
  one test" shape), each with its own comment explaining why; the
  file-level `vi.setConfig` is kept too, now correctly documented as a
  default for any FUTURE test in this file that omits its own explicit
  timeout, not as what fixed the two real failures. **Confirmed green by
  a real full-file run after the actual fix**: `lib/ingestion/__tests__/
  sync.live.test.ts` -- 13 tests, 12 passed + 1 correctly skipped (missing
  optional live-env var, pre-existing), 0 failed, including `dedup is
  per-fill` (27432ms) and `cross-account isolation` (20966ms) -- both
  previously-failing tests now comfortably pass. The one-off
  `shadow_runs_user_id_fkey` violation seen during initial investigation
  (a caught, logged, non-blocking error inside this slice's own try/catch
  -- the sync itself still succeeded even when it fired) did NOT recur
  across two subsequent clean full-file runs, consistent with a transient
  shared-dev-DB artefact (this machine's own well-documented live-DB-
  parallelism contention pattern, not a bug in the new write path) rather
  than a real defect -- flagged for the tester to keep an eye on, not
  treated as resolved-with-certainty by this coder alone. Also re-ran and

- 2026-09-10 -- SECURITY REVIEWER (retrospeq-security-reviewer), SCOPED
  REVIEW of Module 03 §4.8's field-cap warning UI slice (the NEW
  `captured_field_count` join in `fetchStrategiesForUser`,
  `lib/fields/strategy-repository.ts`), dispatched as the last gate
  before this slice moves to qa -> commit. Not a full slice re-audit --
  scoped exactly to the new join per the dispatch's own 5-point list.
  **PASS on all 5 points. Cleared for qa and commit.**

  1. **Cross-user leak/miscount via the `(user_id, id)` composite PK --
     PASS.** Read the live query text directly
     (`lib/fields/strategy-repository.ts` L527-558). The subquery's join
     is `left join retrospeq.fields f on f.user_id = s.user_id and f.id
     = fe.entry->>'field_id'` -- scoped by `f.user_id = s.user_id`, NOT
     by `id` alone, and `s.user_id` is itself constrained to the caller
     by the outer `where s.user_id = $1`. Confirmed this is not merely
     an unenforced application-layer convention: `retrospeq.fields` has
     its own real RLS (`fields_owner_select`,
     `20260902010000_field_registry_schema.sql` L208-211, `using
     (user_id = auth.uid())`), so even a hypothetical join missing the
     `user_id` predicate would still be blocked at the table level for
     any query run under `withUserConnection`. Two independent layers,
     both correct.
  2. **Connection role -- PASS.** `fetchStrategiesForUser` runs under
     `withUserConnection(userId, ...)` (`strategy-repository.ts` L528),
     which does `SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated` + `request.jwt.claims`
     resolving `auth.uid()` to the real caller (`lib/supabase/direct.ts`
     L93-98) -- genuine RLS enforcement, not `withServiceRoleConnection`.
     No service-role path involved; the "service-role allowlist test"
     question in the dispatch doesn't apply here.
  3. **Injection surface -- PASS.** The query is one static SQL string
     with a single bound parameter (`$1` = userId); `jsonb_array_elements`
     and `->>'field_id'` operate on `sv.fields` (server-read jsonb, not
     concatenated user input) with no string-building of SQL text
     anywhere in this function or `rebuildFieldUsagesForStrategy`/
     `insertStrategyAndVersion`/`applyStrategyEditVersion` (also read in
     full as part of this review, since they share the same file and the
     same jsonb-snapshot pattern -- none of them string-interpolate
     caller-controlled values into SQL text either).
  4. **Oracle/leakage via the count itself -- non-issue, confirmed.** The
     count is computed entirely from the CALLING user's own
     `strategy_versions.fields[]` snapshot cross-referenced against that
     SAME user's own `retrospeq.fields` rows (per point 1's scoping) --
     structurally incapable of reflecting another user's data, so there
     is no cross-user signal for a count-based or timing-based oracle to
     extract. No further action needed.
  5. **Test file adequacy --
     `lib/fields/__tests__/strategy-cap-warning.independent-verify.live.test.ts`
     -- PASS, genuinely adversarial, not a rubber stamp.** Read the full
     file, then RAN it live (`npx vitest run
     lib/fields/__tests__/strategy-cap-warning.independent-verify.live.test.ts`)
     rather than trusting the tester's ledger claim alone -- all 6 tests
     passed against the real dev/test Postgres in this session (19.56s,
     0 failures). The cross-user-collision describe block specifically
     constructs the adversarial fixture the dispatch asked about: user A
     and user B each own a REAL `retrospeq.fields` row at the identical
     id string with opposite §4.8 classification (A's =
     `kind='account', data_type='rating'` i.e. captured; B's =
     `data_type='note'` i.e. free), then asserts A's own
     `capturedFieldCount` reflects only A's row (1, not 0). A second test
     in the same block is a direct RLS probe -- `withUserConnection(userA.id,
     ...)` running `select id from retrospeq.fields where id = $1` (no
     `user_id` in the WHERE clause at all) against B's row at the shared
     id, asserting zero rows -- proving RLS itself, not just the
     application-layer join predicate, is what blocks the cross-user
     read. This is a real adversarial fixture (same id, two real rows,
     two real users, opposite classification), not an assertion resting
     on mocked or assumed behavior.

  **Also verified (not explicitly asked, but load-bearing for "cleared
  for commit"):** `app/(app)/strategies/page.tsx` and
  `app/(app)/strategies/actions.ts`'s `fetchStrategyList` derive the
  calling user's id from the server-side session
  (`supabase.auth.getUser()` / `requireSessionAndRateLimit`), never from
  client input, before calling `fetchStrategiesForUser` -- no path for a
  client-supplied user id to reach this query.

  **Verdict: this slice's new join introduces no RLS gap, no injection
  surface, and no cross-user leak -- cleared for `retrospeq-qa` and
  commit.** Nothing in this review blocks the slice.

    - The bigint::int cast fix verified with `typeof capturedFieldCount

      a strict `typeof` check a raw uncast `count(*)` (which round-trips

  **4. Cross-user isolation on the new join — DONE, a genuinely new
  threat model the existing suite didn't cover.** `retrospeq.fields`'
  real primary key is `(user_id, id)`, not a globally unique `id` alone
  (confirmed by reading `20260902010000_field_registry_schema.sql`
  directly) — so two different users CAN legitimately own a field row
  with the identical id string. Constructed exactly that collision: user
  A's field at a shared id is `kind='account', data_type='rating'` (a
  real captured field), user B's field at the SAME id string is
  `data_type='note'` (free per §4.8). Confirmed user A's own
  `capturedFieldCount` reflects ONLY A's own field row (1, the captured
  classification) — if the join had ever cross-matched onto B's row
  instead, A's strategy would have incorrectly read 0. A second, more
  direct RLS probe (`withUserConnection(userA.id, ...)` running a raw
  `select ... from retrospeq.fields where id = $1` for the shared id)
  confirms A's own RLS-scoped session genuinely cannot see B's row at
  all — zero rows returned, not merely "the join clause happens to
  filter it out" — so the isolation holds even if the join's own
  `f.user_id = s.user_id` condition were ever accidentally dropped in a
  future edit.

  **Infra finding, worth recording alongside the existing spawn-flakiness

  documented workaround for `spawn UNKNOWN`) genuinely OOM'd
  (`Fatal process out of memory: Zone`, twice, once even with

  meaningfully less memory (a shared-heap worker rather than 1-2 full

  on this host again. `npm run dev` also hit the same OOM signature once
  before starting successfully on retry with
  `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=1536` — same underlying host-memory

- 2026-09-10 -- CODER (picked up, not redone), Module 03 §4.8's field-cap
  warning UI. The original coder dispatch for this slice was cut off by a
  session-continuity gap after finishing the code but before writing its
  own PROGRESS.md entry, running full verification, or cleaning up its own
  throwaway self-check spec -- picked up per this build's own "verify a
  crashed/interrupted dispatch's work rather than redo it" convention,
  not assumed complete. Confirmed the work itself, not just the intent:
  `lib/fields/strategy-validation.ts` gained `fieldCapWarningMessage`
  (§4.8's literal 3-row warning-copy table -- null under 5, the "~20
  trades" message at 5-6, the "that's a lot to fill in" message at 7+ --
  factored as one pure function so every rendering surface shares the
  exact copy rather than two independently-typed strings drifting apart),
  wired into BOTH `StrategyBuilder.tsx`'s live in-progress count (replacing
  an inline ternary that duplicated the same three sentences) and a new
  per-strategy warning on `app/(app)/strategies/page.tsx` for already-
  saved strategies. `strategy-repository.ts`'s `fetchStrategiesForUser`
  gained a real `capturedFieldCount` column (§4.8: "Counts captured fields
  only. Derived and note fields are free.") via a `left join` against
  `retrospeq.fields` per snapshot entry excluding `kind='derived'`/
  `data_type='note'` -- kept genuinely separate from the pre-existing
  `fieldCount` (a plain, unfiltered `jsonb_array_length`), not a silent
  redefinition of what that column already meant elsewhere. One real,
  self-caught bug in the original dispatch's own work, confirmed still
  present and correctly fixed: Postgres's `count(*)` is `bigint`, which
  the pg driver round-trips as a JS string -- without an explicit `::int`
  cast, `fieldCapWarningMessage`'s own `>= 5`/`>= 7` numeric comparisons
  would have silently misevaluated against a string. A field-snapshot
  entry with no resolvable `retrospeq.fields` row (should not happen --
  fields are archived, never hard-deleted) is deliberately still counted
  rather than dropped, so a join miss can only ever over-count toward the
  warning, never silently hide one -- documented inline on the column's
  own doc comment.

- 2026-09-09 -- CODER, Module 03 (Field Registry & Strategy) §4.5/§6.1's fields management screen (`app/(app)/fields/**`) -- built the list/rename/archive/promote UI plus a standalone field-creation flow at `/fields/new`, the gap this dispatch explicitly named ("the backend for all of this already exists ... a field picker/editor screen ... does not"). Read `03-field-registry-and-strategy.md` in full plus `00-foundation.md` before writing code; grepped for existing precedent first and reused it directly rather than inventing a parallel shape -- `RuleList.tsx`'s per-row busy/error/confirm `RowState` pattern (rename/archive/promote controls, an equal-weight archive-confirm pair for the one genuinely irreversible-in-practice action, a page-level primary `.rq-btn` with every row action `.rq-btn--ghost`) and `StrategyBuilder.tsx`'s derived-field-chips + checkbox-picker conventions were both reused near-verbatim rather than reinvented, and `fetchFieldsForUser`/the whole `createField`/`renameField`/`archiveField`/`promoteField`/`findPromotionCandidates` backend (Slices 03c/03d/03e) was consumed as-is with zero changes to its own logic. One genuine backend gap found and closed additively: no existing read returned an ARCHIVED field or a `strategy_var` field's `ownerStrategyId` (the picker read, `fetchFieldsForUser`, is deliberately active-only and lacks both) -- added `fetchFieldsForManagement` as a second, narrowly-scoped read rather than widening the picker read with an `includeArchived` flag, reasoning documented inline (a flag bolted onto a picker contract is a footgun a future picker caller could pass by mistake and start offering an archived field). Reconciled one thing against the spec that isn't literally in §5's reference markup: §5.2's field-editor markup shows a capture-moment selector, but `createField`'s own input has no such parameter (a capture moment is a property of a field's USAGE within a strategy, `strategy_versions.fields[].capture_moment`, not of the registry row itself) -- omitted here, documented in the component's own header, not silently dropped. Two-primary-button risk (this screen's own free/Pro entitlement split rendering both a page-level Pro-upsell button AND the list's own "Add a field" button) found and closed via an explicit `entitled` prop on `FieldsList`, not left to be caught by qa. Self-checked via a throwaway (written, run, deleted, never committed) Playwright spec against the live dev DB -- full lifecycle for both a free-plan and Pro-plan real user, 9 screenshots read back and confirmed clean against the design-system rules; one real bug the self-check itself caught and fixed (duplicate "Your fields" heading) before handoff. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .`/`npm run build` all clean; full existing `lib/fields` suite (272 tests, 17 files) re-run and confirmed unaffected by the one additive repository change. **Not marked done** -- needs tester (real unit/component/E2E coverage for this slice, which currently has none beyond this session's own deleted throwaway script) -> security-reviewer (this is the first UI surface reading/writing `fields`/`field_usages` directly from a client-triggered flow; worth a fresh look even though the underlying repository functions were already reviewed in Slices 03c/03d/03e) -> qa before commit, per this build's own convention.

- 2026-09-09 -- INFRA FINDING, `chromium_headless_shell` crashes with a genuine fatal exception (`0xe0000008`, not the already-documented ENOSPC download failure) launching against a real dev server on this host -- found during the fields-management screen's own self-check. The existing ENOSPC-workaround entry in Infra gaps (below) already names `chromium-1223` (full, non-headless-shell Chrome, already present at `C:\Users\hp\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1223\chrome-win64\chrome.exe`) as a zero-new-disk-writes substitute for `chromium_headless_shell` -- confirmed here that the SAME substitute also closes this distinct crash mode, not just the disk-space one. Wired as an opt-in `playwright.config.ts` `use.launchOptions.executablePath`, gated behind a new `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROME_PATH` env var (unset by default -- verified zero effect on every OTHER `npx playwright test` invocation in this repo, including the ones this same session ran without it) so the next agent that hits this crash doesn't have to rediscover the workaround or hand-edit the config file themselves; just export `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROME_PATH="C:\Users\hp\AppData\Local\ms-playwright\chromium-1223\chrome-win64\chrome.exe"` before running `npx playwright test`. Also independently re-confirmed this session: an accumulation of orphaned `chrome.exe` processes from an earlier timed-out launch attempt (Playwright's own 30s "setting up page" timeout, hit once while this same crash was first being diagnosed) measurably ate into free host memory and contributed to a SEPARATE V8 heap OOM in the Playwright worker process itself on the next attempt -- `taskkill //F //IM chrome.exe` before retrying is worth doing reflexively whenever a Playwright run on this host was interrupted by a timeout rather than a clean pass/fail, not just after an explicit crash.

     never proposes a rule" -- PASS, confirmed by direct read of
     gates.ts, not re-trusting the prior three reports.
     computeDetection's formula (gates.ts:328) is
     ruleProposable: core.classification === pattern AND core.tier ===
     count_outcome -- a plain boolean AND of two independently-set
     fields, so tier: count forces false regardless of
     classification, and classification: incident forces false
     regardless of tier. No other code path sets ruleProposable for
     the standard path. computeImprovementDetection (gates.ts:441)
     has exactly one non-null return statement, and
     ruleProposable: false is a literal, unconditional boolean in that
     object literal -- no branch, ternary, or later mutation could make
     it anything else. Confirmed unreachable-wrong, not just untested.

  3. Week-boundary spirit ("streak counts weeks, not days") -- PASS.
     isoWeekStart (gates.ts:188) does genuine ISO-calendar-week
     (Monday-start) bucketing via Date.UTC/getUTCDay(), not a naive
     day-count divided by 7 -- confirmed by reading the function body
     directly. It is the ONLY week-bucketing function in this file,
     invoked from a single call site inside computeDetectionCore,
     which BOTH computeDetection (weeks=2) and

     literal, unconditional boolean in that object literal -- there is

     (gates.ts:328) is ruleProposable: core.classification === pattern

  2. Mutual-exclusivity tie-break write-path safety -- PASS, verified
     by reading detection-engine.ts (computeAllImprovementDetections
     ForUser, lines 190-220, "if (standardAnalyticIds.has(analyticId))
     continue") AND repository.ts actual write path together, not
     the pure functions in isolation. computeDetectionsForUserId
     (repository.ts:161-190) calls the standard computation first, then
     passes its same-run results into the improvement computation as
     standardResults -- concatenates [...standardResults,
     ...improvementResults], and because the improvement pass skips any
     analyticId already present in standardResults, the concatenated
     array can never contain two entries for the same analyticId
     within one run. Race between two CONCURRENT recompute calls for the
     SAME user -- read lib/supabase/direct.ts withRole (lines
     56-83) directly: withServiceRoleConnection wraps its entire
     callback in ONE Postgres transaction (begin ... fn ...
     commit), which means writeDetectionsForUser whole per-analytic
     loop -- including every pg_advisory_xact_lock acquired inside it
     -- runs inside that single transaction, so every lock it takes is
     held until the ENTIRE write completes, not released between
     analytics. Two concurrent writeDetectionsForUser calls for the
     same user iterate DETECTION_ANALYTIC_IDS in the same fixed order
     (repository.ts:255), so the second call blocks on the first shared
     (user_id, analyticId) lock until the first call whole
     transaction commits -- this fully serializes the two calls for any
     analytic they both touch, so detections_active_analytic_uidx
     cannot be violated by this race. Confirmed the lock key
     (detections:USERID:ANALYTICID, repository.ts:259) is
     identical regardless of which code path (standard or improvement)
     produced the result being written -- writeDetectionsForUser never
     branches on direction at all, matching its own header claim.

  INFRA NOTE, worth recording since it cost real time this pass: PowerShell's
  `*>` redirect operator is NOT valid Git-Bash syntax -- in this repo's Bash
  tool (POSIX sh), a bare `*>` is parsed as a glob (`*`) immediately followed
  by a `>` redirect, so `npx vitest run <specific-path> *> file.log` silently
  expanded `*` to every top-level file/dir in the repo root and appended them
  ALL as extra positional arguments to vitest -- which broadened what looked
  like a scoped run into an accidental full-repo run every time (explains an
  early false alarm: a "scoped" run showed 209 files/2468 tests, and a
  follow-up attempt adding `--pool=forks --poolOptions.forks.singleFork` on
  top of the same broadened run then genuinely OOM'd this ~16GB-RAM/~5.5GB-
  free machine by serializing dozens of live-DB tests that are normally
  parallelized). Fix: use plain Bash redirection (`> file 2>&1`), not `*>` --
  once corrected, every detection-engine-scoped run finished in ~1-63s with
  zero memory issues. Flagging this explicitly since AGENTS.md's own
  infra-notes template (`C:` `ENOSPC`, etc.) is exactly where a future agent
  hitting the identical false "the suite won't scope/OOMs" conclusion should
  look first, rather than assuming Vitest itself is broken on this host.

- 2026-09-09 -- SECURITY REVIEW (blocking gate), Module 03 strategy-builder
  orphan-cleanup DELETE path -- deleteOrphanedStrategyShell
  (lib/fields/strategy-repository.ts, lines ~878-899) and its wiring into
  createStrategyFromBuilder's catch block (app/(app)/strategies/actions.ts,
  lines ~389-451). Verdict: PASS, no blocking findings. Read the actual
  code (not assumed), read the live migration
  (supabase/migrations/20260902010000_field_registry_schema.sql), and
  re-ran the regression test myself against the live dev/test Postgres
  instance rather than trusting its prior "3 tests pass" claim on faith.

  1. Guard conditions / TOCTOU -- PASS. This is a SINGLE guarded SQL
     DELETE statement (strategy-repository.ts:880-897), not a
     read-then-delete pair -- current_version = 1, is_default = false,
     user_id = $2 are literal columns in the DELETE's own WHERE clause;
     fields = []/triggers = [] are checked via an EXISTS subquery against
     strategy_versions in the SAME statement. Postgres evaluates the whole
     WHERE/EXISTS clause against one consistent read-committed snapshot as
     part of executing this one statement -- there is no window between
     "read to decide" and "delete" for a client (or another request) to
     invalidate. current_version is the one column that actually changes
     when a strategy gains real content (applyStrategyEditVersion's step
     (c)), so once a concurrent editStrategy call COMMITS, this guard
     genuinely stops matching -- verified this is the load-bearing column,
     not the always-true sv.version = 1 / empty-JSONB check (version-1's
     own fields/triggers are immutable per strategy_versions_forbid_mutation,
     so that half of the guard is structurally constant for a genuine
     shell; current_version is what actually gates real-content
     protection). Considered the narrower case of two literally
     concurrent, in-flight (uncommitted) transactions racing for the SAME
     row -- worst case for a same-user self-race (e.g. a second browser
     tab; there is no genuine same-flow concurrency since this function
     only runs sequentially after editStrategy has already failed and
     rolled back) is Postgres's own row-lock serialization causing one
     side to lose and hit the pre-existing "structurally impossible"
     throw/rollback, never silent data loss or a real-content row deleted.
     Not a bypassable window.
  2. Cross-user deletion -- PASS. userId passed to
     deleteOrphanedStrategyShell is always user.id from
     requireSessionAndRateLimit's own supabase.auth.getUser() call
     (actions.ts:327), never client-supplied, and strategyId is always
     shell.strategyId, the id createStrategy itself just returned in the
     SAME request -- there is no code path where a caller passes an
     arbitrary strategyId into this function. Defense in depth confirmed
     at two independent layers: (a) the DELETE's own s.user_id = $2
     literal predicate, (b) real RLS -- deleteOrphanedStrategyShell runs
     inside withUserConnection(userId, ...) (lib/supabase/direct.ts:93-98),
     which sets Postgres role authenticated and
     request.jwt.claims.sub = userId, so strategies_owner's policy
     (using (user_id = auth.uid()) with check (user_id = auth.uid()),
     20260902010000_field_registry_schema.sql:93-96) is genuinely enforced
     by Postgres itself, not merely trusted at the application layer. Even
     a hypothetically-crafted strategyId for another user's real row would
     match neither predicate.
  3. Cleanup-failure fallback / error leakage -- PASS. cleanedUp is false
     for both a thrown cleanup attempt (catch sets it and logs
     server-side only, actions.ts:407-419) and a real non-match
     (rowCount !== 1, strategy-repository.ts:897) -- both paths converge
     on the SAME pre-existing STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL fallback, so a
     failed cleanup can never be mistaken for or mask a different kind of
     failure; the original step-3 error is logged via console.error
     BEFORE the cleanup attempt even starts (actions.ts:395-398), so
     nothing is ever lost even though cleanup never masks anything.
     Every client-facing error object returned to the browser is a
     static, hardcoded user_message string
     (STRATEGY_BUILDER_CREATE_FAILED/STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL/etc,
     actions.ts:425-450) -- grepped every "return { error: ... }" in this
     function and confirmed none interpolates err.message, a raw Postgres
     error, a strategyId, or another user's data into anything sent to
     the client; strategyId/raw err only ever reach console.error
     (server-side log), matching docs/runbook.md's own "grep server logs
     for this prefix" operational guidance.
  4. RLS -- PASS, reuses the established pattern correctly, no new
     migration needed. strategies/strategy_versions/trigger_conditions/
     field_usages all already have "enable row level security" plus a real
     owner policy from Slice 03a
     (20260902010000_field_registry_schema.sql:91-96, 359-376, 453-468,
     501-506) -- confirmed by reading the migration directly, not assumed.
     This new DELETE path adds no new table and needs no new policy; it
     runs through withUserConnection, the SAME genuinely-RLS-enforced
     connection helper every other read/write in this file already uses
     (matching lib/rules/rules-repository.ts's established posture, per
     this file's own header) -- not a service-role bypass anywhere in this
     path. Cascading child deletes (strategy_versions, trigger_conditions,
     field_usages, all "on delete cascade" from strategies) are enforced
     at the FK/DB layer, independent of and in addition to RLS.
  5. Regression test -- PASS, re-ran it myself, live DB, not read-only
     trust. app/(app)/strategies/__tests__/create-strategy-orphan-cleanup.live.test.ts,
     all 3 tests, executed against the real dev/test Postgres instance
     (describe.skipIf(!env), SUPABASE_DB_URL present) via
     "npx vitest run" on this exact file (TEMP/TMP/TMPDIR redirected to
     E:\tmp_vitest, output redirected with PowerShell "*>" to a file per
     the standing C:-full workaround, never piped through tail). Result:
     3 passed / 3, 0 failed, 33.41s. Confirmed the assertions are
     genuinely load-bearing (real db.query COUNT checks against
     retrospeq.strategies/retrospeq.trigger_conditions for the test user,
     not mock call-counts): test 1 proves zero rows remain after a forced
     step-3 failure plus successful compensating delete; test 2 proves the
     shell AND its one real trigger_conditions row are still genuinely
     present (not falsely claimed cleaned up) when the compensating
     delete itself is also forced to fail, falling back to
     STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL; test 3 is a positive control proving the
     ordinary success path (real content, version 2) is unaffected by any
     of this. deleteOrphanedStrategyShell is call-through to the REAL
     implementation by default in this test file (only editStrategy, and
     in test 2 only deleteOrphanedStrategyShell itself, are mocked) --
     this is a genuine live-DB proof, not a mocked assertion dressed up
     as one.
  6. Standard checks -- PASS. No retrospeq.rule_evaluations table touched
     anywhere in this diff (confirmed by grep across
     strategy-repository.ts/actions.ts/trigger-conditions-repository.ts --
     zero hits). No compound-rule shape anywhere in this path (trigger
     conditions are free text with no operand/op/value at all, per
     trigger-conditions-repository.ts's own header and docs/adr/0022 --
     N/A to this diff by construction, not merely unused). All SQL in the
     new DELETE and every surrounding query is parameterized ($1/$2
     positional binds throughout, confirmed by reading every client.query
     call in the touched functions) -- the only string-template usages in
     this file are error-MESSAGE text (StrategyEditConflictError,
     StrategyCreateCapExceededError, etc.), never SQL text, confirmed by
     grep. No eval/new Function anywhere in this file. No XP/adherence
     coupling -- this path never touches retrospeq.rules,
     retrospeq.rule_versions, or anything XP-related.

- 2026-09-09 -- CODER FIX, Module 03 strategy-builder UI --
  `STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL` compensating delete, closing the review
  finding logged immediately below this entry ("STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL
  -- DISAGREE WITH THE ADR'S OWN CONCLUSION"). Read `app/(app)/strategies/
  actions.ts`'s actual `createStrategyFromBuilder`, `lib/fields/
  strategy-repository.ts`'s `createStrategy`/`editStrategy`, and
  `lib/fields/trigger-conditions-repository.ts`'s `createTriggerCondition`
  first, then read `lib/privacy/erasure.ts`, `lib/ingestion/confirm.ts`,
  and `lib/ingestion/split-join.ts` for this build's own established
  partial-write/rollback pattern (atomic guarded UPDATE/DELETE, the
  WHERE clause itself as the safety check), per the review's own
  instruction, before writing anything.

  **What was built:** `deleteOrphanedStrategyShell(userId, strategyId)`
  (`lib/fields/strategy-repository.ts`) -- a single guarded DELETE that
  matches ONLY the exact orphan shape the review (and `docs/runbook.md`'s
  own pre-existing manual-cleanup query) already identified: owned by the
  caller, `current_version = 1`, that version's own `fields`/`triggers`
  JSONB snapshot both empty, and NOT `is_default` (Module 08's future
  silent default strategy is the one legitimate reason a version-1,
  all-empty strategy should exist). Deliberately checks the CURRENT
  version's own JSONB, not whether real `trigger_conditions` rows exist --
  when step 2 (`createTriggerCondition`) succeeds for one or more
  conditions before step 3 (`editStrategy`) fails, those real
  `trigger_conditions` rows are exactly as orphaned as the shell and get
  cleaned up too, via the existing `on delete cascade` FK from
  `trigger_conditions.strategy_id` -- one DELETE, no explicit child
  cleanup needed. `createStrategyFromBuilder`'s own catch block now
  attempts this compensating delete BEFORE surfacing an error: success
  returns a new `STRATEGY_BUILDER_CREATE_FAILED` (nothing left behind, the
  trader is told nothing was saved, which is now actually true); a failed
  cleanup attempt (or a non-match) falls back to the pre-existing,
  honestly-surfaced `STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL` path UNCHANGED -- the
  original error is logged first and is never masked by the cleanup
  attempt's own outcome.

  **Explicitly NOT built, matching what the review itself said was
  correctly out of scope and what docs/adr/0027 already rejected for good
  reasons that still hold**: no cross-repository shared Postgres
  transaction spanning `strategy-repository.ts`/
  `trigger-conditions-repository.ts` (the compensating delete is a much
  smaller, sufficient fix); no general-purpose `archiveStrategy`/
  `deleteStrategy` feature (`deleteOrphanedStrategyShell` has no
  caller-facing API of its own -- callable only from
  `createStrategyFromBuilder`'s own catch block, and its guard makes it
  structurally incapable of matching any strategy with real content).

  **Docs updated, not left stale**: `docs/adr/0027-strategy-builder-two-
  phase-create.md` gets a full "Addendum (2026-09-09)" section recording
  the actual final decision (fix, not just document) and why, without
  rewriting the original historical reasoning. `docs/runbook.md`'s
  `STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL` entry is corrected to describe the new,
  narrower residual case (the compensating delete itself failing, a
  second independent infrastructure failure) rather than the original
  broad gap, including how to distinguish the two cases from the server
  log lines alone.

  **Proof, live-DB, not mocked**: new file
  `app/(app)/strategies/__tests__/create-strategy-orphan-cleanup.live.test.ts`,
  3 tests, following this repo's own established `confirm-day-action.live.test.ts`

- 2026-09-09 -- INDEPENDENT REVIEW (first gate of any kind), Module 03
  (Field Registry & Strategy) strategy list + creation builder UI
  (`app/(app)/strategies/**`, `docs/adr/0027-strategy-builder-two-phase-
  create.md`, `lib/fields/hedge-words.ts`, plus diffs in
  `app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `docs/runbook.md`,
  `lib/fields/{fields,strategy,trigger-conditions}-repository.ts`,
  `lib/rate-limit/config.ts`) -- an out-of-order landing: this slice was
  built by an earlier session before the ledger's own decision to defer
  Module 03 UI in favor of Module 05's detection engine was recorded (see
  "Current task"'s 2026-09-09 RESUME 2 entry, which found this working
  tree shared with the concurrent session that built it, uncommitted,
  with **no tester/security-reviewer/qa gate of any kind having run**).
  This entry is that first gate. Read `00-foundation.md` §9,
  `03-field-registry-and-strategy.md` §§5-9 in full, every file in the
  diff above, and ADR 0027 in full before writing anything below.
  **VERDICT: CONDITIONAL PASS -- functionally correct and well-reasoned,
  but not done. One real, fixable gap (the partial-write cleanup) should
  be closed before this ships, and unit/component test coverage for this
  slice's own new code is genuinely zero going into this review and only
  partially closed by it.**

  1. **Spec fidelity (§5.1/§5.2) -- PASS.** The list (`strategies/page.tsx`)
     and builder (`strategies/new/{page,StrategyBuilder}.tsx`) match the
     spec's own flow (name → triggers → fields → save) and reference
     markup closely: the hedge-word advisory renders exactly the
     spec-mandated example ("Setup looks clean" → "may mean different
     things on different days"), trigger conditions are explicitly
     non-blocking ("These are never enforced. You can always take the
     trade."), derived fields render as static, non-selectable chips
     under "Recorded automatically," and the capture-moment control per
     field -- not shown in §5.2's own field-PICKER markup, only in the
     separate field-EDITOR markup -- is a deliberate, well-documented
     necessary addition (the component's own header explains why: a
     valid submission cannot omit `capture_moment` per field). Field
     CREATION and strategy EDIT/promotion UI are explicitly, correctly
     out of scope with no half-built affordances left dangling (no
     "Add a field" button rendered, matching the code's own honest
     narrowing of §5.2's illustrative markup).
  2. **STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL (docs/adr/0027) -- DISAGREE WITH THE
     ADR'S OWN CONCLUSION. This needs a real (small, scoped) fix, not
     just documentation, before this slice is called done.** The ADR is
     honest and technically correct about the mechanism (three
     sequential, non-transactional calls across two repository files,
     each opening its own `withUserConnection`; a mid-flight failure
     after step 1 leaves a real, permanently orphaned, empty strategy
     with no cleanup path) and correctly narrows the reachable window to
     genuine infrastructure failure via its own pre-validation pass. But
     it then rejects BOTH of two meaningfully different fixes as one:
     (a) a full cross-repository shared-transaction rearchitecture, and
     (b) a general `archiveStrategy`/`deleteStrategy` user-facing feature
     -- both genuinely out of this slice's scope, correctly rejected --
     and stops there. It never considers the much narrower third option:
     a private, internal **compensating delete of the shell strategy
     itself**, called only from `createStrategyFromBuilder`'s own catch
     block when steps 2/3 fail after step 1 committed. This is
     structurally cheap to build correctly: the shell is created with
     `fields: []` and `triggers: []` by construction, so a guarded delete
     (`current_version = 1 AND no version 2 exists AND v1's own
     fields/triggers are both empty AND NOT is_default`) can only ever
     match the exact orphan shape this bug produces, never a real
     trader's strategy -- and the schema's own cascading FKs
     (`fields.owner_strategy_id`, `strategy_versions.strategy_id`, both
     `on delete cascade`) already do the cleanup work for free once that
     one row is deleted, matching the manual recovery step
     `docs/runbook.md`'s own new entry already documents as safe ("delete
     the orphaned `strategies`/`strategy_versions` rows for that specific
     `strategyId`, cascade covers `field_usages` automatically"). This is
     not "build the general delete feature" -- it is "automate the exact
     runbook step already written down, scoped to the one shape that can
     never be a real strategy." Given this build's own established
     discipline on this exact bug class -- PROGRESS.md's own Phase 1
     completion note names concurrency/partial-write races in
     `erasure.ts`, `confirm.ts`, and `split-join.ts` as "all the same bug
     class, all fixed with the same atomic-conditional-UPDATE pattern,"
     not documented and deferred -- treating this one as "genuinely
     requires new backend work out of scope" undersells what's actually
     buildable here. **Recommendation, not implemented by this review
     (implementation is a coder-dispatch action, kept separate from this
     independent-verification pass by design): add a narrow, private
     `deleteEmptyStrategyShell(userId, strategyId)` to
     `strategy-repository.ts`, guarded exactly as above, called from
     `createStrategyFromBuilder`'s catch block before returning
     `STRATEGY_BUILDER_PARTIAL`; if the compensating delete itself fails,
     fall back to today's honest-surfacing behavior unchanged.** Tracked
     as a real, open finding -- not a blocker invented after the fact,
     but a genuine "should be fixed before this is called done" per this
     build's own track record on the identical bug class.
  3. **Entitlement gating (`strategy.create`, ADR 0018) -- PASS, verified
     three ways, not just read.** Confirmed by code reading that all
     three repository entry points `createStrategyFromBuilder` composes
     (`createStrategy`, `editStrategy`, `createTriggerCondition`) each
     independently call `canForUser(userId, 'strategy.create')` before
     any write -- genuine defense-in-depth, not a client-side-only gate.
     Verified this empirically, not just by reading the code: a new live
     test, `lib/fields/__tests__/strategy-builder-entitlement-defense-in-
     depth.live.test.ts` (3 tests, real Supabase test project, real free-
     plan user), proves a free user is rejected with
     `StrategyEntitlementLimitError` and zero rows written at all three
     entry points -- including the specific bypass ADR 0027/this file's
     own header worries about (`createTriggerCondition` called directly
     against a strategy the free user genuinely owns, skipping
     `editStrategy`'s own gate entirely). **All 3 passed** (single-thread
     pool, ~9s). A companion E2E test confirms the UI side: a free user's
     `/strategies` and `/strategies/new` render the Pro-upsell gate only
     -- no builder markup in the DOM at all, not merely hidden -- and
     zero `strategies` rows exist after a full signed-in session that
     never even reaches a save control.
  4. **Wiring correctness -- PASS, verified against real current
     signatures with fresh adversarial fixtures, not just the happy
     path.** Cross-checked every call site
     (`createStrategy`/`editStrategy`/`createTriggerCondition`/
     `fetchFieldsForUser`/`fetchStrategiesForUser`) against the actual
     current exports in `strategy-repository.ts`/`trigger-conditions-
     repository.ts`/`fields-repository.ts` -- all match. Then proved it
     end-to-end with a real DB, not just types: a new E2E spec
     (`e2e/strategies-builder.independent-verify.spec.ts`, 3 tests, real
     Supabase test project + real `next dev` server) walks a Pro user
     through the FULL two-phase path (2 trigger conditions -> the
     `createStrategy` shell -> `createTriggerCondition` x2 -> `editStrategy`
     sequence) and asserts DIRECTLY against Postgres afterward: the
     strategy's `current_version` really is 2 (not 1); `strategy_versions.
     triggers[].condition_id` resolves to REAL `trigger_conditions` rows
     this user owns (never a `"pending-N"` placeholder -- the exact risk
     ADR 0027's own "tension" section reasons about); version 1 (the

  2. Decision: CLOSE the gap now, not re-defer. The 2026-09-08 PASS's own

       -DriveLetter C` showed roughly 26GB free at install time (not 0 --

       rule, `from: { path: '^lib/analytics' }` to `to: { path:
       '^lib/rules', reachable: true }` -- the `reachable: true` option is
       the load-bearing part: it makes this a TRANSITIVE reachability
       check over the real resolved module graph (via `tsConfig.fileName:
       'tsconfig.json'`, so the @/* path alias resolves the same way tsc
       resolves it), not a direct-edge-only check, which is exactly the
       shape needed to catch a re-export indirection through a THIRD file
       outside lib/analytics/**.

       `depcruise --config .dependency-cruiser.cjs lib/analytics`. No CI

       "real mechanism available today, wired into CI the moment CI
       exists" posture for the ESLint half of this same boundary.
     - Verified it actually catches the exact bypass shape, not just that
       it runs clean -- reconstructed the identical fixture the ESLint
       canary test uses (a file under lib/entitlements/ re-exporting
       @/lib/rules/operand-catalogue, consumed by a file under

       json, confirming the tool DID resolve both edges correctly,
       violation.ts -> reexport.ts -> operand-catalogue.ts, but the rule
       wasn't asking a transitive question). Fixed by adding `reachable:
       true` to the `to` restriction (this is dependency-cruiser's actual
       documented mechanism for "not just a direct dependency" -- confirmed
       against the package's own bundled JSON schema,
       ReachabilityForbiddenRuleType, which REQUIRES `reachable` on `to`).
       Re-ran: exit 1, reported the violation with the full intermediate
       chain shown as the `via` path -- this is genuine empirical proof
       the mechanism works, not a config that merely looks right. Deleted

  SECOND TASK -- rule_proposable: MY OWN INDEPENDENT VERDICT, NOT
  DEFERRED TO THE TESTER'S FRAMING.

  3. Re-confirmed the tester's factual claim directly: read types.ts's
     DetectionComputationResult (analyticId, occurrences, windowFrom,
     windowTo, distinctDays, baseRate, outcomeAvgR, outcomeBaselineAvgR,
     tier, classification -- no rule_proposable), gates.ts's
     computeDetection return shape, the detections INSERT column list in
     repository.ts's writeDetectionsForUser (11 named columns, no
     rule_proposable), and the detections table DDL. Confirmed absent
     everywhere, matching the tester's finding exactly.
     Independent trust-boundary assessment (the actual ask of this
     dispatch): NOT currently exploitable, NOT a live security hole today,
     but a real, correctly-flagged completeness gap with genuine future
     security-adjacent stakes. Reasoning:
     - `grep -rln "detections" app/ lib/` (excluding this slice's own
       files/tests) surfaces exactly two hits -- app/(app)/rules/page.tsx
       (prose comments only, "ranked detections leading," no code reading
       the table) and lib/ingestion/sync.ts (the write-side hook itself).
       There is no downstream reader of the detections table anywhere in
       this repo today. Nothing can currently misuse a count-tier row as a
       rule proposal, because nothing currently reads detections at all
       for that purpose. This is the load-bearing fact for my verdict: an
       absent enforcement point is only actively dangerous if something is
       exercising the gap it should be closing, and nothing is.
     - This is NOT the same as saying it's fine to leave alone. Per
       05-analytics-and-findings.md section 5's own prose (verified
       directly, quoted verbatim in the tester's entry below):
       rule_proposable is described as computed centrally in Module 05
       SPECIFICALLY so downstream consumers (Module 06's rule-proposal
       surface, per section 6.1 of the same spec, "provides -> Findings
       and detections with rule_proposable") never have to re-derive the
       two-field conjunction (tier is count_outcome AND classification is
       pattern) themselves. That conjunction IS mechanically recoverable
       from the persisted tier/classification columns today (both columns
       exist, both are correctly computed and tested per the tester's own
       coverage) -- so a careful future implementation COULD reconstruct
       the identical boolean without the dedicated column. But "correctly
       recoverable if implemented exactly right" is a materially weaker
       guarantee than "computed once, centrally, and impossible to get
       subtly wrong per call site" -- and the specific non-negotiable at
       stake here ("the count tier never proposes a rule on frequency
       alone") is exactly the kind of rule where a single
       off-by-one-field mistake in a future Module 06 dispatch (e.g.
       checking classification alone, forgetting the tier half) would
       silently reintroduce the exact failure mode the spec's own prose
       calls out by name.
     - My verdict: this is a security-relevant, spec-mandated enforcement
       gap that I would block a FUTURE consumer's launch on, but I do NOT
       block THIS slice's own security PASS on it, for three reasons:
       (a) it is not currently exploitable -- no live code path exists to
       misuse it; (b) it is explicitly not mine to fix under this
       dispatch's own constraints (adding rule_proposable is production
       logic -- a new computed field plus a migration column -- squarely a
       coder task, not a config/tooling/canary-test-level fix the way the
       ESLint gap was); (c) it has now been recorded TWICE, independently
       (tester's dated entry below, and this entry), and promoted into
       PROGRESS.md's durable Infra-gaps list (not left to live only in a
       decision-log entry that could scroll out of the "current" frame)
       with an explicit binding condition mirroring the ESLint-boundary
       pattern: must be added before any downstream code reads
       detections, so the same "close the loop before the gap becomes
       reachable" discipline applies here as it did to the ESLint gap.

  STANDARD SCOPE (per this dispatch's own checklist).

  5. Injection surface in repository.ts -- PASS, none found. Every query
     is parameterized ($1/$2/... placeholders throughout, including
     pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1::text)) -- the lock key string

     analyticId values written to the tier/analytic_id columns come only
     from DETECTION_ANALYTIC_IDS, a fixed "as const" array declared in

     eval/new Function/string-built SQL anywhere in the five files
     (grepped directly, zero hits).
  6. recomputeDetectionsForUser in sync.ts -- PASS, no credential/

  VERDICT: PASS. No new blocking finding. The one open item

  1. **SECTION 4.7 "NEVER NAME THE SYNDROME" -- PASS, trivially but for a
     real reason, not by omission.** Read every string this slice's code
     can emit or persist: `detection-engine.ts`'s `DETECTION_ANALYTIC_IDS`
     (`seq.reentry_after_loss`, `seq.trades_per_day`,
     `seq.consecutive_losses`, `seq.daily_loss_breach`, `risk.spread` --
     internal ids, never rendered), `repository.ts`'s INSERT column list
     (11 numeric/enum columns, zero free-text/label columns -- `tier`
     is `count`/`count_outcome`, `classification` is `incident`/`pattern`,
     neither is user-facing copy), and every doc-comment across
     `gates.ts`/`occurrence-detectors.ts`/`types.ts` (code comments only,
     confirmed via `grep -rli` for `detection-engine app/` returning
     nothing -- this module renders nothing yet, matching Module 05's own
     "This module renders nothing. It supplies typed payloads,"
     independently re-confirmed by me, not just trusted from the tester's
     identical finding). Grepped the whole
     `lib/analytics/detection-engine/` directory case-insensitively for
     `revenge|tilt|FOMO|undisciplined|syndrome`: zero hits. The one place
     informal language DOES appear is an internal code comment only
     (`occurrence-detectors.ts:153`, "Overtrading days." -- a
     section-header comment, never a persisted or rendered string) -- not
     user-facing, does not violate this rule as written, but flagging the
     phrase itself for whoever writes Module 06/08's actual rendering copy
     so "overtrading" doesn't get carried verbatim into a user-facing
     string later without going through the count-and-outcome framing the
     non-negotiable requires.
  2. **NO RED/GREEN -- PASS.** Grepped `lib/analytics/detection-engine/`
     for hex codes, `rgb(`, and `red|green|success|danger` (case-
     insensitive): zero color/severity-encoding hits of any kind (matches
     found were all prose words like "guarantee"/"predate"/"configured
     rule," not color tokens). This slice has no UI surface and no
     severity-to-color mapping anywhere in its own code -- `tier`/
     `classification` are plain enums, never paired with a color constant.
  3. **ADR 0029 SUPERSESSION KEY -- VERIFIED AGAINST ACTUAL CODE, not
     just trusted from the ADR's own prose.** ADR 0029 claims: key is
     `(user_id, analytic_id)`, enforced by a non-deferrable partial unique
     index (`detections_active_analytic_uidx`), write pattern is
     supersede-then-insert as two separate statements inside one
     `pg_advisory_xact_lock`-guarded transaction (never a combined CTE),
     and a gate-failed analytic's prior `active` row is left untouched.
     Read `repository.ts`'s `writeDetectionsForUser` line by line: it
     locks on the full `(userId, analyticId)` tuple string, then issues an
     UPDATE (state to superseded, where user_id/analytic_id/state=active)
     strictly BEFORE the INSERT (values ending in 'active'), in that
     literal statement order, inside the same client transaction --
     matches the ADR exactly. The loop iterates `DETECTION_ANALYTIC_IDS`
     (the fixed constant list) and skips (continue) any analytic absent
     from this run's `results` -- confirming a gate-failed analytic is
     never touched at all, not superseded, not re-inserted, exactly as
     ADR 0029's "Consequences" section states. Read the migration
     (20260909030000) directly: it creates exactly one unique index on
     (user_id, analytic_id) where state = active -- the literal partial
     unique index the ADR describes, no ALTER TABLE, no RLS/policy touch
     (confirmed -- the migration's only other statement is the
     analytic_config seed insert). Independently re-ran the live
     supersession test myself rather than trusting the tester's report of
     it (vitest against this slice's six test files plus the shared
     week-boundary cross-check file, TEMP/TMP/TMPDIR redirected to
     E:\tmp_vitest per this repo's own documented workaround): 6 test
     files, 77 passed, 1 intentionally skipped, 0 failed -- byte-for-byte
     matching the tester's own reported count, not just similar. Also
     independently re-ran the import-boundaries check script: "no
     dependency violations found (78 modules, 179 dependencies cruised)",
     confirming the security-reviewer's ESLint-boundary closure claim is
     real and still green today, not stale. ADR 0029's claim and the
     actual code genuinely match -- no gap found.
  4. **SYMMETRIC HANDOFF CHECK -- PASS, both entries present and
     consistent with expectations, nothing missing.** Read both 2026-09-09
     entries in full before doing anything else. The SECURITY REVIEWER
     entry (topmost at dispatch time) documents exactly what this
     dispatch expected: dependency-cruiser 16.10.4 installed (pinned

     over.
  5. **GENERAL DRIFT CHECK -- PASS.** No currency P&L anywhere in this
     slice's output -- DetectionComputationResult carries no money field
     at all (realizedPnl is read internally by
     computeDailyLossBreachOccurrences purely to derive a PERCENT-of-

     conclusion as tester/security-reviewer, not taken on faith.** Ran my

     not a live violation of the non-negotiable today. Do not let Module

     directly without that column existing first.
  7. **DOCUMENTATION (00-foundation section 12) -- PASS.** Both ADRs
     present, in docs/adr/, non-placeholder, and independently verified
     against the actual code (item 3 above) rather than assumed correct
     from their own prose. docs/runbook.md's "Detection engine
     `detections` recompute failing after a sync" entry is present, real,
     specific (names the exact log-line prefix, distinguishes a genuine

     leaving these five unseeded would make this entire slice's real,

  8. **PERFORMANCE (00-foundation section 8.1) -- PASS, no obvious
     budget-breaker.** fetchAccountsForUser/fetchEligibleTradesByAccount
     are each ONE query for the whole user (not per-account, not N+1) --
     confirmed by reading repository.ts directly. The five detectors run
     in-memory over already-fetched rows, no further DB round-trips. The
     existing trades_user_day index (user_id, server_day desc) covers the
     where-user_id predicate this slice's own query uses; the
     order-by-account_id/opened_at clause doesn't match that index's own
     column order, so a sort step is likely, but this is a background,
     best-effort, non-blocking post-sync recompute with no stated latency
     budget in the performance table (unlike pre-entry capture/rule
     preview), and mirrors the already-reviewed-and-accepted edge-engine
     repository sibling's identical shape -- not a new pattern introduced
     here. No synchronous call to anything that should be precomputed;
     this recompute IS the precomputation step for a future Module 06
     consumer.

- 2026-09-09 -- TESTER GATE, Module 05 detection engine (`lib/analytics/

  production files under test.

  VERDICT: PASS on everything I could test with the tools available, with
  **two real, concerning findings** that are NOT mine to fix as tester
  (reported, not silently patched) -- see items 5 and 6 below. Do not read
  this as an unconditional "slice is done" -- item 5 in particular is a
  genuine spec-vs-code gap in a hard non-negotiable-adjacent rule.

  **New files (6, all under this dispatch, fresh fixtures, none reused
  from the coder's own scenarios):**
  - `lib/analytics/detection-engine/__tests__/gates.test.ts` (28 tests --
    unit + adversarial coverage of the three gates, classification,
    tier logic, `mergeAccountSummaries`, `isoWeekStart`)
  - `lib/analytics/detection-engine/__tests__/gates.property.test.ts` (6

  - `lib/analytics/detection-engine/__tests__/occurrence-detectors.test.ts`
    (32 tests -- unit coverage of all five §4.5 detectors)
  - `lib/analytics/detection-engine/__tests__/detection-engine.test.ts` (5
